The vessels entered as &quot;River Steamers&quot; are, for the most part, steam-launches under so tons which tow Native boats. The carrying of cargo employs about half a dozen larger vessels built for river work, but only two of these are over x50 tons. The smaller launches cannot be classed as ocean-going vessels; they only run on the Canton and West Rivers.

PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended September 80 1898.
192 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. (JULY-*SEPT. NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 325. LIGHTS, ETC., TO BE EXHIBITED BY WRECK-MARKING BOATS. NOTICE is hereby given that in future the distinguishing sign of a wreck-marking boat will at night consist of either one red light or a red light with a white light vertically below it. By day such a boat will exhibit a red flag or such other mark as may be locally notified. SmNGiAI, 14th July 1898. No. 826. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NORTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. DRINKWATER POINT LIGHTHOUSE. NOTICE is hereby given that the light at Drinkwater Point was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 15th instant. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, group-flashing, of the fourth order, showing double white flashes at intervals of 2o seconds. The lighthouse stands 995 feet N. 45* W. from the site of the Drinkwater Point Beacon, and the light, which is elevated 89 feet above the level of the sea, shuld be visible in clear weather at a distance of I5 nautical miles. The tower is an iron screw pile structure, So feet high, with a total height from the base to the lantern vane of 931 feet. The tower is painted in alternate black and white horizontal bands. Approximate position:- Latitude ........... ** 31027&#39;So&quot;N. Longitude . . . . . . .... . 121 48&#39; 45&quot; E, S&amp;amp;wana, i6th August 1898.
1898.] NOTIFICATIONS. 193 No. 327. YANGTZE RIVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. NORTH TREE BEACON LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the North Tree Beacon Light has been shifted 94o feet N. 4oo E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. SHANGHAI, 6th September 1898. No. 328. KIUNGCHOW DISTRICT. EAST COAST OF HAINAN: &quot;LORNE ROCK&quot; DISCOVERED. NOTICE is hereby given that the rock on which the s.s. Lorne struck and foundered in I888 has been discovered and is in the following position From the rock- Mount Tonkon bears . . . N. 4. 25&#39; W., magnetic. Chunlan Fort ,.. N. 710 25&#39; W., The rock is a pinnacle rising out of 30 fathoms and is covered 6 feet at low water. It is about 6o feet long in a N.N.E. and S.S.W. direction and about i feet wide. SHANGHAI, 6th September 1898. No. 329. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. DRINKWATER POINT BEACON DISCONTINUED. NOTICE is hereby given that, owing to the establishment of the Drinkwater Point Lighthouse, the Drinkwater Point Beacon has been removed. SHANGHAI, 22nd September 1898. 25

APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JULY-SEPTEMBER 1898. 27
210 CUSTOMS GAZEFTE. [J 4Y-SEPT., (Received i8th October 1 898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, 14TH OCTOBER 1898. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the third quarter of the year 1898, July-September. b. The Custom House (-X f U) was opened on the 2nd April 1887, and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-fei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.A 30 and Hk.M 8o per picul) commenced on the 14th April r887, and the collection of Native Duties on General. Cargo,.at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are known as the Capsuimoon (t * ), Changehow (g o), Fotochow (ijb g), and Kowloon (1 B) stations. c. The word &quot;Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. 11. 1898.] LAPPA. 221 (Received 2oth October 1898.) CUSTOM HOUSE, LAPPA, 12TH OCTOBER 1898. SIR, L-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statisticl Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the third quarter -of the year 1898, July-September. b. The Custom House (A [ ) was opened on the 2nd April 1887. The collection of Tariff Import Duty .and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.M 30 and Hk.?h So per picul) and the collection of Provincial Likin and Ching-te Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, com- menced on that day. The collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Native Custom House &#39;Tariff received from the Hoppo, commenced on the Ist July 1887. The stations at present controlled by the Lappa Customs are known as the Malowchow ( M M ) and *Chienshan (I iJ) stations, with three sub-stations, known, -as the Shekkok ( A), Kuttai (t k), and Kwanchiap (U ) sub-stations. c. The word &quot; Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
